# u. proof of proposition 33: projection onto  $\bar{\mathcal{l}}$

proof: we first analyze the constraints defined by the affine subspace  $\bar{\mathcal{l}}$  (83). for any matrix  $\bar{m} \in \bar{\mathcal{l}}$ ,  $\bar{m}$  can be written as  $\bar{m} = \bar{q} - \hat{\mu} j + \delta$ , with  $\delta \in \mathcal{h}$  and  $\bar{m}$  satisfies  $\bar{m}\bar{x} = 0$ , we write this in matrix form:

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c c c c c c} - \hat {\mu} \mathbf {i} _ {4} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} & [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 1} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 1} & \dots & [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} & \dots & [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} \\ {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 1} - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 1}} & {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {1 1} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {1 1}} & \dots & {[ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {1 k}} & \dots & {[ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {1 k}} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k}} & - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {1 k} & \dots & {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k}} & \dots & {[ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k}} \\ \vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k}} & - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {1 k} & \dots & - [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} & \dots & {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} + [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k}} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} e \\ \hat {\theta} _ {1} e \\ \vdots \\ \hat {\theta} _ {k} e \\ \vdots \\ \hat {\theta} _ {k} e \end{array} \right] = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {0} \\ \mathbf {0} \\ \vdots \\ \mathbf {0} \\ \vdots \\ \mathbf {0} \end{array} \right], (1 2 8)
$$

where the expressions for  $[\bar{\mathbf{q}}]_{0k}$  and  $[\bar{\mathbf{q}}]_{kk}$  are given by the following:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} {\left[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} \right] _ {0 k} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} - \left[ \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{4} \left(\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \bar {c} ^ {2}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} + \frac {1}{2} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \mathbf {i} _ {3} - \frac {1}{2} \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \left[ \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} - \frac {1}{2} \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} & \frac {1}{2} \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} \\ \frac {1}{2} \left(\left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k}\right) ^ {\mathsf {t}} & \frac {1}{4} \left(\left\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right\| ^ {2} - \bar {c} ^ {2}\right) \end{array} \right]} \\ {[ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} - 2 [ \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} (\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \bar {c} ^ {2}) \mathbf {i} _ {3} + \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \mathbf {i} _ {3} - [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} - \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} & [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} \\ (\left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k}) ^ {\mathsf {t}} & \frac {1}{2} (\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \bar {c} ^ {2}) \end{array} \right]} \\ , \forall k = 1, \dots , k. \end{array} \right. (1 2 9)
$$

denote  $\hat{\theta}_0 = +1$ , we write the  $k$ -th  $(k = 1, \dots, k)$  block-row equality of (128):

$$
\left(\hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} + \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i}\right) \boldsymbol {e} = \mathbf {0} \tag {130}
$$

$$
\leftrightarrow \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \star & \hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} \\ \star & \hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {s} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} + \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} \end{array} \right] \boldsymbol {e} = \mathbf {0} \tag {131}
$$

$$
\leftrightarrow \left\{ \begin{array}{l} {\left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {k k} ^ {v} = - \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left[ \boldsymbol {\bar {q}} \right] _ {0 k} ^ {v} - \left[ \boldsymbol {\bar {q}} \right] _ {k k} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {k i} ^ {v} = - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \boldsymbol {\hat {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {k i} ^ {v}} \\ {\left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {k k} ^ {s} = - \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left[ \boldsymbol {\bar {q}} \right] _ {0 k} ^ {s} - \left[ \boldsymbol {\bar {q}} \right] _ {k k} ^ {s} = - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2}} \end{array} , \right. \tag {132}
$$

and from eq. (132), we can get the a sum of all  $[\delta]_{kk}^{v}$  to be:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} \\ = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \delta ] _ {0 k} ^ {v}, \tag {133} \\ \end{array}
$$

and the sum of all  $[\delta]_{kk}^{s}$  to be:

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2}. \tag {134}
$$

now we write the 0-th block row equality of (128):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(- \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\mu} \mathbf {i} _ {4} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k}\right) \boldsymbol {e} = \mathbf {0} (135) \\ \leftrightarrow \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \star & - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} \\ \star & - \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\mu} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {s} \end{array} \right] \boldsymbol {e} = \mathbf {0} (136) \\ \leftrightarrow \left\{ \begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} \\ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} = - \hat {\mu} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} ] _ {0 k} ^ {s} \end{array} \right. (137) \\ \leftrightarrow \left\{ \begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} \\ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} = - \hat {\mu} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} (\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \bar {c} ^ {2}) \end{array} , \right. (138) \\ \end{array}
$$

and compare eq. (138) with eq. (133):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \delta ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \delta ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} (139) \\ \leftrightarrow \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} (140) \\ \leftrightarrow \sum_ {\hat {\theta} _ {k} = + 1} - \frac {3}{2} [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} + \sum_ {\hat {\theta} _ {k} = - 1} - \frac {1}{2} [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} = \sum_ {\hat {\theta} _ {k} = + 1} \frac {1}{2} [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} + \sum_ {\hat {\theta} _ {k} = - 1} - \frac {1}{2} [ \hat {\xi} _ {k} ] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} (141) \\ \leftrightarrow \sum_ {\hat {\theta} _ {k} = + 1} \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k} = \mathbf {0}, (142) \\ \end{array}
$$

which states that for inliers  $\hat{\theta}_k = +1$ , the sum  $\sum_{\hat{\theta}_k = +1}[\hat{\pmb{\xi}}_k] \times \bar{\pmb{a}}_k = \mathbf{0}$  must hold<sup>12</sup>. we then compare eq. (138) with eq. (134):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2} = - \hat {\mu} + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left(\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \bar {c} ^ {2}\right) (143) \\ \leftrightarrow \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2} = - \left(\frac {1}{2} (\hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} (1 - \hat {\theta} _ {k}) \bar {c} ^ {2}\right) + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \left(\| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \bar {c} ^ {2}\right) (144) \\ \leftrightarrow \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2}, (145) \\ \end{array}
$$

which holds true without any condition. therefore, we conclude that the equality constraint of the 0-th block row adds no extra constraints on the matrix  $\pmb{\delta}$ . this leads to the following theorem.

theorem 34 (equivalent projection to  $\bar{\mathcal{l}}$ ). the projection of a matrix  $m$  onto  $\bar{\mathcal{l}}$  is equivalent to:

$$
\pi_ {\bar {\mathcal {l}}} (\boldsymbol {m}) = \pi_ {\bar {\mathcal {h}}} (\boldsymbol {m} - \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} + \hat {\mu} \boldsymbol {j}) + \bar {\boldsymbol {q}} - \hat {\mu} \boldsymbol {j}, \tag {146}
$$

where  $\bar{\mathcal{h}}$  is defined by:

$$
\bar {\mathcal {h}} \doteq \{\delta : \delta \in \mathcal {h}, \delta \text {s a t i s f i e s} \quad (1 3 2) \} \tag {147}
$$

next we prove that the projection steps in proposition 33 define the correct projection  $\pi_{\bar{\mathcal{h}}}$ . to this end, we use  $\bar{h}$  to denote the projection of a matrix  $h \in s^{4(k+1)}$  onto the set  $\bar{\mathcal{h}}$ . by the definition of projection,  $\bar{h}$  is the minimizer of the following optimization:

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {h}} = \underset {\delta \in \bar {\mathcal {h}}} {\arg \min } \| \boldsymbol {h} - \boldsymbol {\delta} \| _ {f} ^ {2}. \tag {148}
$$

denoting the objective function of eq. (148) as  $f = \| h - \delta \|_f^2$ , we separate the frobenius norm of  $h - \delta$  into a sum of block-wise frobenius norms:

$$
f = \| \boldsymbol {h} - \boldsymbol {\delta} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \tag {149}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {m} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {m} \| _ {f} ^ {2}} _ {f _ {k k} ^ {m}: \text {d i a g o n a l b l o c k m a t r i x p a r t}} + \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} \| _ {f} ^ {2}} _ {f _ {k k} ^ {s}: \text {d i a g o n a l b l o c k s c a l a r p a r t}} + \underbrace {2 \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {l} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {m} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {m} \| _ {f} ^ {2}} _ {f _ {\mathcal {z}} ^ {m}: \text {o f f - d i a g o n a l b l o c k m a t r i x p a r t}} + \underbrace {2 \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {l} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {s} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {s} \| _ {f} ^ {2}} _ {f _ {\mathcal {z}} ^ {s}: \text {o f f - d i a g o n a l b l o c k s c a l a r p a r t}} \\ + 2 \underbrace {\left(\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} \| _ {f} ^ {2} + \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {l} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {v} \| _ {f} ^ {2} + \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {l} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} \| _ {f} ^ {2}\right)} _ {f ^ {v}: \text {d i a g o n a l a n d o f f - d i a g o n a l v e c t o r p a r t}}, \tag {150} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathcal{w}$  is the following set of  $l$  ordered indices that enumerate the lower-triangular blocks of any matrix  $\delta \in s^{4(k + 1)}$ :

$$
\mathcal {w} = \{(1, 0), \dots , (k, 0), (2, 1), \dots , (k, 1), \dots , (k, k - 1) \}, \tag {151}
$$

and  $\mathcal{w}(l)$  equals to the flipped copy of  $\mathcal{z}(l)$  (eq. (91)). using the separated frobenius norm (150), we can get the projection  $\bar{\pmb{h}}$  block by block in the following steps.

this condition is always true because this is the first-order stationary condition for the tls optimization considering only the least squares part applied on inliers.

1) diagonal block matrix part. from the expression of  $f_{kk}^{m}$  and the constraint that  $\sum_{k=0}^{k}[\delta]_{kk}^{m} = 0$ , we can get the diagonal block matrix part of  $\bar{h}$  to be:

$$
[ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} ] _ {k k} ^ {m} = [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {m} - \frac {\sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {m}}{k + 1}, \quad \forall k = 0, \dots , k, \tag {152}
$$

which first computes the mean of all diagonal block matrix parts,  $\sum_{k=0}^{k}[\pmb{h}]_{kk}^{m}/(k+1)$ , and then subtracts the mean from each individual diagonal block matrix part  $[\pmb{h}]_{kk}^{m}$ .

2) diagonal block scalar part. from the expression of  $f_{kk}^{s}$  and the constraint from eq. (132) (which fixes the scalar parts to be known constants), we can get the diagonal block scalar part of  $\bar{h}$  to be:

$$
[ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} ] _ {k k} ^ {s} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} + \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2} & k = 1, \dots , k, \\ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + \frac {1}{2}\right) \| \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \| ^ {2} - \left(\frac {1}{4} \hat {\theta} _ {k} - \frac {1}{2}\right) \bar {c} ^ {2} & k = 0. \end{array} \right., \tag {153}
$$

which first computes the diagonal scalar parts for  $k = 1, \ldots, k$  and then assigns the negative sum of all diagonal scalar parts to the scalar part of the top-left diagonal block  $([\bar{h}]_{00}^{s})$ .

3) off-diagonal block matrix part. from the expression of  $f_{\mathcal{z}}^{m}$  and the constraint that  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}$  is skew-symmetric, we can obtain the off-diagonal block matrix part of  $\bar{h}$  to be:

$$
[ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {m} = \frac {[ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {m} - \left([ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {m}\right) ^ {\intercal}}{2}, \quad \forall l = 1, \dots , l, \tag {154}
$$

which simply projects  $[\pmb{h}]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^m$  to its nearest skew-symmetric matrix. note that it suffices to only project the upper-triangular off-diagonal blocks because both  $\pmb{h}$  and  $\bar{\pmb{h}}$  are symmetric matrices.

4) off-diagonal block scalar part. the off-diagonal blocks are skew-symmetric matrices and the scalar parts must be zeros. therefore, the off-diagonal block scalar parts of  $\bar{h}$  are:

$$
\left[ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} \right] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {s} = 0, \quad \forall l = 1, \dots , l. \tag {155}
$$

5) off-diagonal block vector part. the projection of the vector parts of each block is non-trivial due to the coupling between the diagonal block vector parts  $[\delta]_{kk}^{v}$  and the off-diagonal block vector parts  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^{v}, [\delta]_{\mathcal{w}(l)}^{v}$ . we first write the diagonal block vector parts as functions of the off-diagonal block vector parts by recalling eq. (132) and (133):

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} {\left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {k k} ^ {v}} = \underbrace {- \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k}} _ {:= \phi_ {k}} - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} = - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} + \phi_ {k} \\ {\left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {0 0} ^ {v}} = - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \hat {\boldsymbol {\xi}} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {k}} _ {:= \phi_ {0}} - \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {0} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 k} ^ {v} = - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq 0} \hat {\theta} _ {i} \hat {\theta} _ {k} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {0 i} + \phi_ {0} \end{array} , \right. \tag {156}
$$

which shows that we can write  $[\delta]_{kk}^v, k = 0, \ldots, k$  in a unified notation:

$$
[ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} + \phi_ {k}, \quad \forall k = 0, \dots , k. \tag {157}
$$

inserting eq. (157) into the expression of  $f^v$  in eq. (150), and using the fact that  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{w}(l)}^v = -[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^v$ , we obtain the following unconstrained optimization problem:

$$
\left. \min  _ {[ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v}} \sum_ {l = 1} ^ {l} \left\| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} \right\| ^ {2} + \left\| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {v} \right\| ^ {2} + \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k} \left\| \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} - \phi_ {k} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} \right\| ^ {2}. \right. \tag {158}
$$

to solve problem (158), we first derive the partial derivatives w.r.t. each variable  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^v$ . for notation simplicity, we write  $\mathcal{z}(l) = (r_l, c_l)$ , where  $r_l < c_l$  represent the block-row and block-column indices. in addition, we use  $u(r, c)$  to denote

the pointer that returns the index of  $(r,c)$  in the set  $\mathcal{z}$ . using this notation, the partial derivative of  $f^v$  w.r.t.  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^v$  is:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial f ^ {v}}{\partial [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v}} = \frac {\partial \left(\left. \begin{array}{c} \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} \| ^ {2} + \| [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {v} \| ^ {2} + \\ \| \sum_ {i = 0 , i \neq r _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l} , i} ^ {v} - \phi_ {r _ {l}} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {r _ {l}, r _ {l}} ^ {v} \| ^ {2} + \| \sum_ {i = 0 , i \neq c _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \phi_ {c _ {l}} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {c _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {v} \| ^ {2} \end{array} \right)\right)}{\partial [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v}} (159) \\ = 2 \left(\hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \left(\sum_ {\substack {i = 0 \\ i \neq r _ {l}}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \phi_ {r _ {l}} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {r _ {l}, r _ {l}} ^ {v}\right) - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \left(\sum_ {\substack {i = 0 \\ i \neq c _ {l}}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \phi_ {c _ {l}} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {c _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {v}\right)\right) (160) \\ = 2 \left(4 [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {u} + \sum_ {i \neq r _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i \neq r _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} + \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \left(\phi_ {c _ {l}} - \phi_ {r _ {l}} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {r _ {l}, r _ {l}} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {c _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {v}\right)\right). (161) \\ \end{array}
$$

notice that not all of the  $[\delta]_{rl,i}^{v}$  and  $[\delta]_{cl,i}^{v}$  in eq. (161) belong to the upper-triangular part of  $\delta$ , therefore, we use the fact that  $[\delta]_{ij}^{v} = -[\delta]_{ji}^{v}$  for any  $i \neq j$  to convert all lower-triangular vectors to upper-triangular. in addition, we set the partial derivative to zero to obtain sufficient and necessary conditions to solve the convex unconstrained optimization (158):

$$
\begin{array}{l} 4 [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i \neq r _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i \neq r _ {l}, c _ {l}} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} = \underbrace {[ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {\mathcal {w} (l)} ^ {v} + \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} (\phi_ {r _ {l}} - \phi_ {c _ {l}} - [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {r _ {l} , r _ {l}} ^ {v} + [ \boldsymbol {h} ] _ {c _ {l} , c _ {l}} ^ {v})} _ {: := \mathcal {f} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {h})} (162) \\ \leftrightarrow 4 \left[ \boldsymbol {\delta} \right] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i <   r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l}, i} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i > r _ {l}, i \neq c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {r _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i > c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {c _ {l}, i} ^ {v} = \mathcal {f} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {h}) (163) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leftrightarrow 4 [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {\mathcal {z} (l)} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i <   r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {u (i, r _ {l})} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i > r _ {l}, i \neq c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {u (r _ {l}, i)} ^ {v} + \sum_ {i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {u (i, c _ {l})} ^ {v} - \sum_ {i > c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \boldsymbol {\delta} ] _ {u (c _ {l}, i)} ^ {v} = \mathcal {f} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {h}) (164) \\ \leftrightarrow \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \mathcal {c} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) = \mathcal {f} _ {l} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\boldsymbol {h}), (165) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the linear map  $\mathcal{c}: s^{4(k+1)} \to \mathbb{r}^{l \times 3}$  is as defined in eq. (92), and  $\mathcal{f}_l(\pmb{h}) \in \mathbb{r}^3$  is defined as in eq. (162), and  $\pmb{a}_l \in \mathbb{r}^l$  is the following vector:

$$
\boldsymbol {a} _ {l} (u) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 4 & \text {i f} u = l \\ - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & \text {i f} u = u (i, r _ {l}), \forall 0 \leq i <   r _ {l} \\ \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & \text {i f} u = u (r _ {l}, i), \forall r _ {l} <   i \leq k, i \neq c _ {l} \\ \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & \text {i f} u = u (i, c _ {l}), \forall 0 \leq i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l} \\ - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & \text {i f} u = u (c _ {l}, i), \forall c _ {l} <   i \leq k \\ 0 & \text {o t h e r w i s e} \end{array} , \right. \tag {166}
$$

where  $\mathbf{a}_l(u)$  denotes the  $u$ -th entry of vector  $\mathbf{a}_l$ . because the partial derivative w.r.t. each variable  $[\delta]_{\mathcal{z}(l)}^v, l = 1, \dots, l$ , must be zero at the global minimizer,  $\bar{\mathbf{h}}$ , of problem (158), we have  $l$  linear equations of the form eq. (165), which allows us to write the following matrix form:

$$
\underbrace {\left[ \boldsymbol {a} _ {1} , \dots , \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} , \dots , \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} \right] ^ {\mathrm {t}}} _ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathcal {s} ^ {l}} \underbrace {\mathcal {c} (\bar {\boldsymbol {h}})} _ {\in \mathbb {r} ^ {l \times 3}} = \underbrace {\left[ \mathcal {f} _ {1} (\boldsymbol {h}) , \dots , \mathcal {f} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {h}) , \dots , \mathcal {f} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {h}) \right] ^ {\mathrm {t}}} _ {\mathcal {f} (\boldsymbol {h}) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {l \times 3}} \tag {167}
$$

and we can compute  $\mathcal{c}(\bar{\pmb{h}})$  by:

$$
\mathcal {c} (\bar {\boldsymbol {h}}) = \boldsymbol {a} ^ {- 1} \mathcal {f} (\boldsymbol {h}). \tag {168}
$$

surprisingly, due to the structure of  $a$ , its inverse can be computed in closed form.

theorem 35 (closed-form matrix inverse). the inverse of matrix  $a$  (166), denoted  $p$ , can be computed in closed form, with each column of  $p$  being:

$$
\boldsymbol {p} _ {l} (u) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} p _ {1} & \text {i f} u = l \\ \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} & \text {i f} u = u (i, r _ {l}), \forall 0 \leq i <   r _ {l} \\ - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} & \text {i f} u = u (r _ {l}, i), \forall r _ {l} <   i \leq k, i \neq c _ {l} \\ - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} & \text {i f} u = u (i, c _ {l}), \forall 0 \leq i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l} \\ \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} & \text {i f} u = u (c _ {l}, i), \forall c _ {l} <   i \leq k \\ 0 & \text {o t h e r w i s e} \end{array} , \right. \tag {169}
$$

where  $p_1$  and  $p_2$  are the following constants:

$$
p _ {1} = \frac {k + 1}{2 k + 6}, \quad p _ {2} = \frac {1}{2 k + 6}. \tag {170}
$$

proof: to show  $\pmb{p}$  is the inverse of  $\pmb{a}$ , we need to show  $\pmb{ap} = \mathbf{i}_l$ . to do so, we first show that  $\pmb{a}_l^\top \pmb{p}_l = 1$ , which means the diagonal entries of  $\pmb{ap}$  are all equal to 1. note that from the definition of  $\pmb{a}_l$  in (166) and  $\pmb{p}_l$  in (169), one can see that there are  $2(k - 1) + 1$  non-zero entries in  $\pmb{a}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_l(u) = -p_2\pmb{a}_l(u)$  when  $u \neq l$ , and  $\pmb{p}_l(u) = p_1$ ,  $\pmb{a}_l(u) = 4$  when  $u = l$ , therefore, we have:

$$
\boldsymbol {a} _ {l} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {p} _ {l} = 4 p _ {1} - 2 (k - 1) p _ {2} = \frac {4 k + 4}{2 k + 6} - \frac {2 k - 2}{2 k + 6} = \frac {2 k + 6}{2 k + 6} = 1. \tag {171}
$$

we then show that  $\mathbf{a}_l^\top \mathbf{p}_m = 0$  for any  $l \neq m$ . the non-zero indices of  $\mathbf{a}_l$ ,  $\mathbf{p}_m$  and their corresponding entries are:

$$
\begin{array}{c c c c} u & \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} (u) & u & \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} (u) \\ \hline u \left(r _ {l}, c _ {l}\right) & 4 & u \left(r _ {m}, c _ {m}\right) & p _ {1} \\ u \left(i, r _ {l}\right), \forall 0 \leq i <   r _ {l} & - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & u \left(i, r _ {m}\right), \forall 0 \leq i <   r _ {m} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \\ u \left(r _ {l}, i\right), \forall r _ {l} <   i \leq k, i \neq c _ {l} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & u \left(r _ {m}, i\right), \forall r _ {m} <   i \leq k, i \neq c _ {m} & - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \\ u \left(i, c _ {l}\right), \forall 0 \leq i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l} & \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & u \left(i, c _ {m}\right), \forall 0 \leq i <   c _ {m}, i \neq r _ {m} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \\ u \left(c _ {l}, i\right), \forall c _ {l} <   i \leq k, & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & u \left(c _ {m}, i\right), \forall c _ {m} <   i \leq k, & \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \end{array} , \tag {172}
$$

where we have used  $\mathcal{z}(l) = (r_l, c_l)$  and  $\mathcal{z}(m) = (r_m, c_m)$ . now depending on the 12 different relations between  $(r_l, c_l)$  and  $(r_m, c_m)$ ,  $a_l$  and  $p_m$  can have different ways of sharing common indices whose corresponding entries in both  $a_l$  and  $p_m$  are non-zero. we next show that in all these 12 different cases,  $a_l^\top p_m = 0$  holds true.

a)  $r_l < c_l < r_m < c_m$ :  $\pmb{a}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_m$  share 4 non-zero entries with the same indices:

$$
\begin{array}{c c c} u & \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} (u) & \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} (u) \\ \hline u \left(r _ {l}, r _ {m}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(r _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2}. \\ u \left(c _ {l}, r _ {m}\right) & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(c _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} p _ {2} \end{array} . \tag {173}
$$

using the above table, we easily see that  $\mathbf{a}_l^\top \mathbf{p}_m = 0$ .

b)  $r_l < c_l = r_m < c_m$ :  $a_l$  and  $p_m$  share the following common indices whose corresponding entries are non-zero:

$$
\begin{array}{c c c} u & \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} (u) & \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} (u) \\ \hline u \left(r _ {l}, c _ {l}\right) & 4 & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(r _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(i, c _ {l}\right), \forall 0 \leq i <   c _ {l}, i \neq r _ {l} & \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \\ u \left(c _ {l}, i\right), \forall c _ {l} <   i \leq k, i \neq c _ {m} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} & - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {i} p _ {2} \\ u \left(c _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & p _ {1} \end{array} \tag {174}
$$

using the table above, we can calculate  $a_{l}^{\top}p_{m}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} = 4 \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} p _ {2} + (c _ {l} - 1) \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} p _ {2} + (k - c _ {l} - 1) \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} p _ {2} - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} p _ {1} \\ = \left(\left(k + 1\right) p _ {2} - p _ {1}\right) \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} = 0, \tag {175} \\ \end{array}
$$

which ends up being 0 due to  $p_1 = (k + 1)p_2$  (cf. eq. (170)).

c)  $r_l < r_m < c_l < c_m$ :  $a_l$  and  $p_m$  share 4 common indices with non-zero entries:

$$
\begin{array}{c c c} u & \boldsymbol {a} _ {l} (u) & \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} (u) \\ \hline u \left(r _ {l}, r _ {m}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(r _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(r _ {m}, c _ {l}\right) & \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} p _ {2} \\ u \left(c _ {l}, c _ {m}\right) & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} & - \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} p _ {2} \end{array} \tag {176}
$$

from which we can compute that  $a_l^\top p_m = 0$ .

d)  $r_l = r_m < c_l < c_m$ :  $\pmb{a}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_m$  share the following common indices with non-zero entries:

<table><tr><td>u</td><td>a_l(u)</td><td>p_m(u)</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_l, c_l)</td><td>4</td><td>-θc_mθclp2</td></tr><tr><td>u(i, r_l), ∀0 ≤ i &lt; r_l</td><td>-θc_lθ_i</td><td>θc_mθi p2</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_l, i), ∀r_l &lt; i ≤ k, i ≠ cl, cm</td><td>θc_lθ_i</td><td>-θc_mθi p2</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_l, cm)</td><td>θc_lθ_c_m</td><td>p1</td></tr><tr><td>u(cl, cm)</td><td>-θ_r_lθ_c_m</td><td>-θ_r_kθ_clp2</td></tr></table>

from which we can compute  $\pmb{a}_l^\top \pmb{p}_m$ :

$$
\boldsymbol {a} _ {l} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} = (- 4 p _ {2} - r _ {l} p _ {2} - (k - r _ {l} - 2) p _ {2} + p _ {1} + p _ {2}) \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} = (p _ {1} - (k + 1) p _ {2}) \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {c _ {m}} = 0 \tag {178}
$$

e)  $r_l < r_m < c_l = c_m$ :  $\pmb{a}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_m$  share the following common indices with non-zero entries:

<table><tr><td>u</td><td>a_l(u)</td><td>p_m(u)</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_l, c_l)</td><td>4</td><td>-θ_r_m θ_r_l p2</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_l, r_m)</td><td>θ_c_l θ_r_m</td><td>θ_c_l θ_r_l p2</td></tr><tr><td>u(i, c_l), ∀0 ≤ i &lt; c_l, i ≠ r_l, r_m</td><td>θ_r_l θ_i</td><td>-θ_r_m θ_i p2</td></tr><tr><td>u(r_m, c_l)</td><td>θ_r_l θ_r_m</td><td>p1</td></tr><tr><td>u(c_l, i) ∀c_l &lt; i ≤ k</td><td>-θ_r_l θ_i</td><td>θ_r_m θ_i p2</td></tr></table>

from which we compute  $a_l^\top p_m$ :

$$
\boldsymbol {a} _ {l} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {p} _ {m} = (- 4 p _ {2} + p _ {2} - (c _ {l} - 2) p _ {2} + p _ {1} - (k - c _ {l}) p _ {2}) \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} = (p _ {1} - (k + 1) p _ {2}) \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {l}} \hat {\theta} _ {r _ {m}} = 0. \tag {180}
$$

f)  $r_m < r_l < c_l < c_m$ :  $\pmb{a}_l$  and  $\pmb{p}_m$  share 4 common indices with non-zero entries:

<table><tr><td>u</td><td>al(u)</td><td>pm(u)</td></tr><tr><td>u(rm,rl)</td><td>-θclθrm</td><td>-θcmθcmθrlp2</td></tr><tr><td>u(rl,cm)</td><td>θclθcm</td><td>-θrmθrlp2</td></tr><tr><td>u(rm,cl)</td><td>θrlθrm</td><td>-θcmθclp2</td></tr><tr><td>u(cl,cm)</td><td>-θrlθcm</td><td>-θrmθclp2</td></tr></table>

from which we easily see that  $a_l^\top p_m = 0$ .

g)  $r_m < r_l < c_l = c_m$ : same as  $r_l < r_m < c_m = c_l$  by symmetry (switch  $m$  and  $l$ ).  
h)  $r_m < r_l < c_m < c_l$ : same as  $r_l < r_m < c_l < c_m$  by symmetry.  
i)  $r_m < r_l = c_m < c_l$ : same as  $r_l < r_m = c_l < c_m$  by symmetry.  
j)  $r_m < c_m < r_l < c_l$ : same as  $r_l < c_l < r_m < c_m$  by symmetry.  
k)  $r_l < r_m < c_m < c_l$ : same as  $r_m < r_l < c_l < c_m$  by symmetry.  
1)  $r_m = r_l < c_m < c_l$ : same as  $r_l = r_m < c_l < c_m$  by symmetry.

as we show above, in all cases,  $a_{l}p_{m} = 0$ , when  $l \neq m$ . therefore, we prove that  $\mathbf{a}\mathbf{p} = \mathbf{i}_l$  and  $\mathbf{p}$  is the closed-form inverse of  $\mathbf{a}$ .

therefore, using theorem 35, we can first generate  $\pmb{p}$  directly (using eq. (169)) and then apply eq. (168) to obtain the off-diagonal block vector parts of  $\bar{h}$ .

6) diagonal block vector part. after obtaining off-diagonal block vector parts, we can insert them back into eq. (157) to obtain the diagonal block vector parts:

$$
[ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} ] _ {k k} ^ {v} = - \sum_ {i = 0, i \neq k} ^ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {k} \hat {\theta} _ {i} [ \bar {\boldsymbol {h}} ] _ {k i} ^ {v} + \phi_ {k}, \tag {182}
$$

$$
\phi_ {k} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} - \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \hat {\xi} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} & k = 1, \dots , k \\ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \left(\frac {1}{2} \hat {\theta} _ {k} + 1\right) \left[ \hat {\xi} _ {k} \right] _ {\times} \bar {a} _ {k} & k = 0 \end{array} . \right. \tag {183}
$$

# v. initial guess for algorithm 3

we describe the initial guess  $\bar{m}_0$  as:

$$
\bar {m} _ {0} = \bar {q} - \hat {\mu} j + \delta_ {0}, \tag {184}
$$

where  $\pmb{\delta}_0\in \mathcal{h}$  is the following:

off-diagonal blocks:  $[\pmb{\delta}]_{\mathcal{u}(l)} = \mathbf{0},\forall l = 1,\dots ,l$  (185)

diagonal block scalar part:  $[\pmb{\delta}]_{kk}^{s} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} - (\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} + \frac{1}{2})\| \hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}\|^{2} + (\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} - \frac{1}{2})\bar{c}^{2} & k = 1,\dots ,k,\\ \sum_{k = 1}^{k}(\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} + \frac{1}{2})\| \hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}\|^{2} - (\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} - \frac{1}{2})\bar{c}^{2} & k = 0. \end{array} \right.$

diagonal block vector part:  $[\pmb{\delta}]_{kk}^{v} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} - \left(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\theta}_{k} + 1\right)[\hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}]_{\times}\bar{\pmb{a}}_{k} & k = 1,\dots ,k,\\ \sum_{k = 1}^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}\hat{\theta}_{k} + 1\right)[\hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}]_{\times}\bar{\pmb{a}}_{k} & k = 0 \end{array} \right.$

diagonal block matrix part:  $[\pmb{\delta}]_{kk}^{m} = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} - [\bar{\pmb{q}} ]_{0k} - \left(\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} + \frac{1}{4}\right)\| \hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}\|^{2}\mathbf{i}_{3} - \frac{1}{2}\bar{c}^{2}\mathbf{i}_{3} & k = 1,\ldots ,k\\ \sum_{k = 1}^{k}\left([\bar{\pmb{q}} ]_{0k} + \left(\frac{1}{4}\hat{\theta}_{k} + \frac{1}{4}\right)\| \hat{\pmb{\xi}}_{k}\|^{2}\mathbf{i}_{3} + \frac{1}{2}\bar{c}^{2}\mathbf{i}_{3}\right) & k = 0 \end{array} \right.$ . (188)

one can verify that  $\delta_0$  satisfies all the constraints defined by set  $\bar{\mathcal{h}}$  (147).

# w. tighter bounds for theorem 17

in this section, we improve over the bounds presented in theorem 17 and derive tighter but more complex bounds for scale, rotation, and translation estimation.

theorem 36 (estimation contract with noisy inliers and adversarial outliers). assume (i) the set of correspondences contains at least 3 distinct and non-collinear inliers, where for any inlier  $i$ ,  $\| b_i - s^\circ \pmb{r}^\circ \pmb{a}_i - t^\circ \| \leq \beta_i$ , and  $(s^\circ, \pmb{r}^\circ, t^\circ)$  denotes the ground truth transformation. assume (ii) the inliers belong to the maximum consensus set in each subproblem<sup>13</sup>, and (iii) the second largest consensus set is "sufficiently smaller" than the maximum consensus set (as formalized in lemma 23). if (iv) the rotation subproblem produces a valid certificate (as per theorems 13-14), then the output  $(\hat{s}, \hat{\pmb{r}}, \hat{t})$  of teaser satisfies:

$$
\left| \hat {s} - s ^ {\circ} \right| \leq \min  \left\{\zeta_ {i} ^ {s}, i \in \mathcal {i} \right\}, \tag {189}
$$

$$
s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \leq \frac {\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i}} \left(\zeta_ {i} ^ {r}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \| _ {f} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {s} \left(\sigma_ {1} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}) + \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}})\right)}, \tag {190}
$$

$$
\left| \hat {t} _ {l} - t _ {l} ^ {\circ} \right| \leq \min  \left\{\left(\left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right)\right) \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \| ^ {2} + \left[ \zeta_ {i} ^ {t} \right] _ {l}: i \in \mathcal {i} \right\}, \forall l = 1, 2, 3. \tag {191}
$$

where  $\mathcal{i}$  is the set of ground-truth inliers with size  $n_i$ , i.e., ground-truth trims in the scale bound, ground-truth tims in the rotation bound, and ground-truth correspondences in the translation bound;  $\hat{t}_l, t_l^\circ, l = 1,2,3$ , denotes the  $l$ -th entry of the translation vector;  $\zeta_i^s, \zeta_i^r$  and  $[\zeta_i^t]_l$  are defined as follows:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} \zeta_ {i} ^ {s} = \left| s _ {i} - \hat {s} \right| + \alpha_ {i} \\ \zeta_ {i} ^ {r} = \frac {\left\| \overline {{\boldsymbol {b}}} _ {i} - \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \overline {{\boldsymbol {a}}} _ {i} \right\|}{\left\| \overline {{\boldsymbol {a}}} _ {i} \right\|} + \alpha_ {i} \\ \left[ \zeta_ {i} ^ {t} \right] _ {l} = \left| \left[ \boldsymbol {b} _ {i} \right] _ {l} - \left[ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} - \hat {t} _ {l} \right| + \beta_ {i} \end{array} , \forall i \in \mathcal {i}, \forall l = 1, 2, 3; \right. \tag {192}
$$

$\theta_{r}$  is the angular (geodesic) distance between  $\hat{\pmb{r}}$  and  $\pmb{r}^{\circ}$ ;  $\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}} = \left[\frac{\bar{a}_{i_1}}{\|\bar{a}_i\|},\dots,\frac{\bar{a}_{i_{n_i}}}{\|\bar{a}_{n_i}\|}\right]\in \mathbb{r}^{3\times n_i}$  is the matrix that assembles all normalized tims in the set  $\mathcal{i}$ , and  $\sigma_{1}(\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}})$ ,  $\sigma_{2}(\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}})$  denotes the smallest and second smallest singular values of matrix  $\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}}$ . note that since there are at least 3 non-collinear ground-truth inliers,  $\mathrm{rank}(a_{\mathcal{i}})\geq 2$  and  $\sigma_{1}(a_{\mathcal{i}}) + \sigma_{2}(a_{\mathcal{i}}) > 0$ , meaning the rotation bound is well defined. since the bounds rely on the knowledge of the inliers  $\mathcal{i}$ , to compute the worst-case bounds, one has to enumerate over all possible subsets of  $n_{i} = 3$  inliers to compute the largest bounds for scale, rotation and translation.

proof: we will prove the bounds for scale, rotation, and translation separately.

scale error bound. let us denote with  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}$  the consensus set associated with the optimal solution of the tls scale estimation problem, with  $|\bar{\mathcal{c}}| = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{in}}$ . to establish a connection with the ground-truth scale  $s^{\circ}$ , we partition  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}$  into two sets:  $\mathcal{i}$  that contains all the indices of the true inliers, and  $\mathcal{j}$  that contains all the indices of the false inliers, which are not generated from the true inlier model (trim) where classified as inliers by tls and ended up in  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}$ . let  $n_{i} = |\mathcal{i}|$  and  $n_{j} = |\mathcal{j}|$ , and we have  $n_{i} + n_{j} = \bar{n}_{\mathrm{in}}$ . for scale measurements that are in  $\mathcal{i}$  and  $\mathcal{j}$ , we have the following relations:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l l} s _ {i} = s ^ {\circ} + \epsilon_ {i} = \hat {s} + \phi_ {i} & \forall i \in \mathcal {i} \\ s _ {j} = \hat {s} + \phi_ {j} & \forall j \in \mathcal {j} \end{array} , \right. \tag {193}
$$

where  $\phi_i$ 's and  $\phi_j$ 's are residuals that can be computed from  $s_i, s_j$ , and  $\hat{s}$ , and  $\epsilon_i$ 's are unknown noise terms. note that we have  $|\epsilon_i| \leq \alpha_i, |\phi_i| \leq \alpha_i, |\phi_j| \leq \alpha_j$ . from the first equation in eq. (193), we have:

$$
\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ} = \epsilon_ {i} - \phi_ {i} \rightarrow | \hat {s} - s ^ {\circ} | \leq | \phi_ {i} | + \alpha_ {i} \dot {=} \zeta_ {i} ^ {s}, \forall i \in \mathcal {i}, \tag {194}
$$

$$
\rightarrow | \hat {s} - s ^ {\circ} | \leq \min  \left\{\zeta_ {i} ^ {s}, i \in \mathcal {i} \right\} \tag {195}
$$

13in other words: the inliers belong to the largest set  $s$  such that for any  $i,j\in s$ ,  $|s_{ij} - s|\leq \alpha_{ij}$ ,  $\| \bar{\pmb{b}}_{ij} - sr\bar{\pmb{a}}_{ij}\| \leq \delta_{ij}$ , and  $|[b_i - sra_i - t]_l|\leq \beta_i,\forall l = 1,2,3$  for any transformation  $(s,r,t)$ . note that for translation the assumption is applied component-wise.

where we have defined a new quantity  $\zeta_i^s = |\phi_i| + \alpha_i$ , that can be computed for every trim in the consensus set  $\bar{c}$ . eq. (194) states that the scale error is bounded by the minimum  $\zeta_i$  in the set of true inliers. therefore, if we know what the set of true inliers  $\mathcal{i}$  is, then we can simply take the minimum of the set  $\{\zeta_i, i \in \mathcal{i}\}$ . however, if we do not know the true inlier set, assuming  $n_i \geq 3$ , we get the worst-case bound:

$$
| \hat {s} - s ^ {\circ} | \leq \zeta_ {m _ {3}} ^ {s}, \tag {196}
$$

where  $\zeta_{m_3}$  is the third-largest  $\zeta_k^s$  in the set  $\{\zeta_k^s = |s_k - \hat{s}| + \alpha_k : k \in \bar{c}\}$ .

rotation error bound. similar to the previous case, we use  $\bar{c}$  to denote the consensus set of tim at the optimal tls estimate. we also use  $\mathcal{i}$  to denote the set of true inliers and use  $\mathcal{j}$  to denote the set false inliers, i.e., outliers that tls selected as inliers. we have the following equations for the true inliers and false inliers:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \bar {\boldsymbol {b}} _ {i} = s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} + \epsilon_ {i} = \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} + \phi_ {i} & \forall i \in \mathcal {i} \\ \bar {\boldsymbol {b}} _ {j} = \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {j} + \phi_ {j} & \forall j \in \mathcal {j} \end{array} , \right. \tag {197}
$$

where  $\phi_i, \phi_j$  are the residuals that can be computed and  $\epsilon_i$  is the unknown noise term. from the first equation of eq. (197), we have:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} = \epsilon_ {i} - \phi_ {i} \leftrightarrow \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \frac {\bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i}}{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} \|} = \frac {\epsilon_ {i} - \phi_ {i}}{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} \|} (198) \\ \rightarrow \| \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \boldsymbol {u} _ {i} \| \leq \frac {\| \phi_ {i} \|}{\| \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {i} \|} + \alpha_ {i} \doteq \zeta_ {i} ^ {r}, \forall i \in \mathcal {i}, (199) \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\pmb{u}_i\stackrel {\cdot}{=}\frac{\bar{a}_i}{\|\bar{a}_i\|}$  and we have defined a new quantity  $\zeta_i^r\stackrel {\cdot}{=}||\phi_i|| / ||\bar{a}_i|| + \alpha_i$  that can be computed from the tls solution. since eq. (199) holds for every tim in  $\mathcal{i}$ , we can aggregate all inequalities:

$$
\left\| \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i}} \left(\zeta_ {i} ^ {r}\right) ^ {2}, \tag {200}
$$

where  $\mathbf{a}_{\mathcal{i}} = [\pmb{u}_{i_1},\dots,\pmb{u}_{i_{n_i}}]\in \mathbb{r}^{3\times n_i},i_1,\dots,i_{n_i}\in \mathcal{i}$ , assembles all normalized tims  $\pmb{u}_i$  in the set  $\mathcal{i}$ . now we want to get a lower bound on  $\| (\hat{s}\hat{\pmb{r}} -s^{\circ}\pmb{r}^{\circ})\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}}\| _f^2$  so that we can upper-bound the rotation error.

towards this goal, we first analyze the singular values of  $(\hat{s}\hat{r} - s^{\circ}r^{\circ})$ . let the three singular values of  $(\hat{s}\hat{r} - s^{\circ}r^{\circ})$  be  $\sigma_{l}, l = 1,2,3$ , then we know that  $\sigma_{l}^{2}, l = 1,2,3$ , are equal to the eigenvalues of the matrix  $b = (\hat{s}\hat{r} - s^{\circ}r^{\circ})^{\top}(\hat{s}\hat{r} - s^{\circ}r^{\circ})$ . expanding  $b$ , we get:

$$
\boldsymbol {b} = \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\intercal} - s ^ {\circ} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {\intercal}\right) \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) = \left(\hat {s} ^ {2} + \left(s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} - \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(\hat {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} + \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {\intercal} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}}\right) = \left(\hat {s} ^ {2} + \left(s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} - \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {s} + \boldsymbol {r} _ {s} ^ {\intercal}\right) (2 0 1)
$$

where we have denoted  $\pmb{r}_{s} = \hat{\pmb{r}}^{\top}\pmb{r}^{\circ} \in \mathrm{so}(3)$ , which is the composition of two rotation matrices. let the axis-angle representation of  $\pmb{r}_{s}$  be  $(\phi_s, \theta_r)$ , then the axis-angle representation for  $\pmb{r}_{s}$  is simply  $(\phi_s, -\theta_r)$ . according to the rodrigues' rotation formula, we can write  $\pmb{r}_{s}$  and  $\pmb{r}_{s}^{\top}$  as:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {s} = \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} + \sin \left(\theta_ {r}\right) \left[ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \right] _ {\times} + \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} ^ {\top} \\ \boldsymbol {r} _ {s} ^ {\top} = \cos (- \theta_ {r}) \mathbf {i} _ {3} + \sin (- \theta_ {r}) \left[ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \right] _ {\times} + \left(1 - \cos (- \theta_ {r})\right) \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} ^ {\top} = \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} - \sin \left(\theta_ {r}\right) \left[ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \right] _ {\times} + (1 - \cos (\theta_ {r})) \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right. \tag {202}
$$

inserting eq. (202) back into eq. (201), we have:

$$
\boldsymbol {b} = \left(\hat {s} ^ {2} + \left(s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} - \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(2 \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right) \mathbf {i} _ {3} + 2 \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {s} ^ {\top}\right), \tag {203}
$$

from which we can see that the three eigenvectors of  $b$  are  $\phi_s$  and  $\phi_1^\perp$  and  $\phi_2^\perp$ , where we pick  $\phi_1^\perp$  and  $\phi_2^\perp$  to be 2 unit orthogonal vectors in the plane that is orthogonal to  $\phi_s$ :

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {b} \phi_ {s} = \left(\left(\hat {s} ^ {2} + (s ^ {\circ}) ^ {2}\right) - 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ}\right) \phi_ {s} = (\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}) ^ {2} \phi_ {s} \\ \boldsymbol {b} \phi_ {l} ^ {\perp} = \left(\hat {s} ^ {2} + (s ^ {\circ}) ^ {2} - 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \phi_ {l} ^ {\perp}, \forall l = 1, 2 \end{array} \right.. \tag {204}
$$

therefore, we have  $\sigma_{1} = \sigma_{2} = \hat{s}^{2} + (s^{\circ})^{2} - 2\hat{s}s^{\circ}\cos (\theta_{r}) = (\hat{s} -s^{\circ})^{2} + 2\hat{s}s^{\circ}(1 - \cos (\theta_{r}))$  and  $\sigma_3 = (\hat{s} -s^\circ)^2$  . let the svd of  $(\hat{s}\hat{r} -s^{\circ}\pmb{r}^{\circ})$  be  $(\hat{s}\hat{r} -s^{\circ}\pmb{r}^{\circ}) = \pmb{u}_{\delta}\pmb{s}_{\delta}\pmb{v}_{\delta}^{\top}$  , we lower-bound  $\| (\hat{s}\hat{r} -s^{\circ}\pmb{r}^{\circ})\pmb{a}_{\mathcal{i}}\| _f^2$  ..

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {s} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \left\| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \left\| \left[ \begin{array}{c c c} \sigma_ {1} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & \sigma_ {2} & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & \sigma_ {3} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{l} \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {1} \\ \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {2} \\ \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {3} \end{array} \right] \right\| _ {f} ^ {2}. (205) \\ = \left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \left(\| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {1} \| ^ {2} + \| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {2} \| ^ {2} + \| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {3} \| ^ {2}\right) + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \left(\| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {1} \| ^ {2} + \| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {2} \| ^ {2}\right) (206) \\ = \left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \left(\| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} - \| (\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}) _ {3} \| ^ {2}\right) (207) \\ \geq \left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \left(\sigma_ {1} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}) + \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}})\right), (208) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used  $\| v_{\delta}a_{\mathcal{i}}\| _f^2 = \| a_{\mathcal{i}}\| _f^2 = \sigma_1^2 (a_{\mathcal{i}}) + \sigma_2^2 (a_{\mathcal{i}}) + \sigma_3^2 (a_{\mathcal{i}})$ , with  $\sigma_{1}(a_{\mathcal{i}})\leq \sigma_{2}(a_{\mathcal{i}})\leq \sigma_{3}(a_{\mathcal{i}})$  being the three singular values of  $a_{\mathcal{i}}$  in ascending order. the last inequality in eq. (208) follows from:

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) _ {3} \right\| ^ {2} = \left\| \left[ \begin{array}{l l l} 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{array} \right] \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \leq \sigma_ {3} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}) \left\| \left[ \begin{array}{l l l} 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{array} \right] \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \sigma_ {3} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}). \tag {209}
$$

now we combine eq. (208) and (200) to bound  $1 - \cos(\theta_r)$ :

$$
\left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \left(\sigma_ {1} ^ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right) + \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}\right)\right) \leq \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i}} \left(\zeta_ {i} ^ {r}\right) ^ {2} \tag {210}
$$

$$
\leftrightarrow s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right) \leq \frac {\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i}} \left(\zeta_ {i} ^ {r}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}} \| _ {f} ^ {2}}{2 \hat {s} \left(\sigma_ {1} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}}) + \sigma_ {2} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {\mathcal {i}})\right)}, \tag {211}
$$

where we note that (i) the existence of at least 3 non-collinear inliers guarantees that  $\mathrm{rank}(a_{\mathcal{i}})\geq 2$  , which means  $\sigma_1^2 (a_\mathcal{i}) + \sigma_2^2 (a_\mathcal{i}) > 0$  and the upper bound is well-defined; (ii) since  $(\phi_s,\theta_r)$  is the axis-angle representation for  $\hat{\pmb{r}}^{\mathrm{t}}\pmb{r}^{\circ}$ $\theta_r$  is the angular (geodesic) distance between  $\hat{\pmb{r}}$  and  $r^{\circ}$  ; (iii) the rotation bound is dependent on the scale bound  $|\hat{s} -s^{\circ}|$  (195).

again, when the true inlier set  $\mathcal{i}$  is known, one can directly compute the rotation bound using eq. (211); when the true inlier set  $\mathcal{i}$  is unknown, one has to enumerate over all possible subsets of 3 tims (formed by a triplet of  $(i,j,k)$  correspondences) in  $\bar{c}$  to compute the worst-case bound.

translation error bound. let  $\pmb{t}^{\circ} = [t_{1}^{\circ}, t_{2}^{\circ}, t_{3}^{\circ}]^{\top}$  and  $\hat{\pmb{t}} = [\hat{t}_{1}, \hat{t}_{2}, \hat{t}_{3}]^{\top}$  be the ground-truth and estimated translation. let  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}_l, l = 1, 2, 3$ , be the set of measurements  $(\pmb{a}_k, \pmb{b}_k)$  that pass the  $l$ -th component-wise adaptive voting, i.e.,  $|[\pmb{b}_k - \hat{s}\hat{r}\pmb{a}_k - \hat{t}]_l| \leq \beta_k, \forall k \in \bar{\mathcal{c}}_l, l = 1, 2, 3$ . again, we let  $\mathcal{i}_l$  and  $\mathcal{j}_l$  be the set of true and false inliers within  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}_l, l = 1, 2, 3$ . from condition (ii), we have that  $\mathcal{i}_1 = \mathcal{i}_2 = \mathcal{i}_3 = \mathcal{i}$ , which says that the true inliers pass all three component-wise adaptive voting. however,  $\mathcal{j}_l, l = 1, 2, 3$ , could potentially be different. then we write the following relations:

$$
\left\{ \begin{array}{l} \left[ \boldsymbol {b} _ {i} \right] _ {l} = \left[ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} + \hat {t} _ {l} + \left[ \phi_ {i} \right] _ {l} = \left[ s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} + t _ {l} ^ {\circ} + \left[ \epsilon_ {i} \right] _ {l} \quad \forall i \in \mathcal {i}, l = 1, 2, 3 \\ [ ] [ \boldsymbol {b} _ {i} ] _ {l} = \left[ \hat {\mathrm {s h o n}} _ {i} ^ {\circ} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {i} \right] _ {l} + \hat {\mathrm {s h o n}} _ {i} ^ {\circ} [ t _ {l} ] \end{array} , \right. \tag {212}
$$

$$
\left\lfloor \left[ \boldsymbol {b} _ {j} \right] _ {l} = \left[ \hat {s} \boldsymbol {\bar {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {j} \right] _ {l} + \hat {t} _ {l} + \left[ \phi_ {j} \right] _ {l} \quad \forall j \in \mathcal {j} _ {l}, l = 1, 2, 3 \right. ^ {\prime}
$$

where  $\epsilon_{i}$  is the unknown noise that satisfies  $\| \epsilon_{i}\| \leq \beta_{i}$  and  $[\phi_i]_l$  and  $[\phi_j]_l$  are the residuals that can be computed from the tls estimate and satisfy  $|[ \phi_i ]_l | \leq \beta_i$  and  $|[ \phi_j ]_l | \leq \beta_j$ . from the first equation in (212), we have that:

$$
\hat {t} _ {l} - t _ {l} ^ {\circ} = \left[ s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} - \left[ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} + \left[ \boldsymbol {\epsilon} _ {i} \right] _ {l} - \left[ \phi_ {i} \right] _ {l} \tag {213}
$$

$$
\rightarrow | \hat {t} _ {l} - t _ {l} ^ {\circ} | \leq | [ s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ] _ {l} - [ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ] _ {l} | + [ \phi_ {i} ] _ {l} + \beta_ {i} = | [ s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ] _ {l} - [ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ] _ {l} | + [ \zeta_ {i} ^ {t} ] _ {l}, \tag {214}
$$

where we have defined a new quantity  $[\zeta_i^t ]_l = [\phi_i]_l + \beta_i$  that can be computed from the solution. now we want to upper-bound  $|[s^{\circ}\pmb{r}^{\circ}\pmb{a}_{i}]_{l} - [\hat{s}\pmb{r}\pmb{a}_{i}]_{l}|$ , using a similar svd-based approach as in eq. (208) (but this time we upper-bound):

$$
\left(\left[ s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l} - \left[ \hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right] _ {l}\right) ^ {2} \leq \left\| \left(\hat {s} \hat {\boldsymbol {r}} - s ^ {\circ} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\circ}\right) \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right\| ^ {2} = \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {s} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right\| ^ {2} = \left\| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\delta} \boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \right\| ^ {2} \tag {215}
$$

$$
= (\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}) ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \| ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} (1 - \cos (\theta_ {r})) \left(\left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i}\right) _ {1} ^ {2} + \left(\boldsymbol {v} _ {\delta} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} _ {i}\right) _ {2} ^ {2}\right) \leq \left(\left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right)\right) \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \| ^ {2} \tag {216}
$$

inserting the inequality (216) back into eq. (214), we get the following translation bound for each  $i \in \mathcal{i}$

$$
\left. \right.\left| \hat {t} _ {l} - t _ {l} ^ {\circ} \right| \leq \left(\left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right)\right) \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \| ^ {2} + \left[ \zeta_ {i} ^ {t} \right] _ {l}, \forall i \in \mathcal {i}, \forall l = 1, 2, 3, \tag {217}
$$

there the final translation bound is:

$$
\left. \left| \hat {t} _ {l} - t _ {l} ^ {\circ} \right| \leq \min  \left\{\left(\left(\hat {s} - s ^ {\circ}\right) ^ {2} + 2 \hat {s} s ^ {\circ} \left(1 - \cos \left(\theta_ {r}\right)\right)\right) \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \| ^ {2} + \left[ \zeta_ {i} ^ {t} \right] _ {l}: i \in \mathcal {i} \right\}, \forall l = 1, 2, 3. \right. \tag {218}
$$

we note that the translation bound depends on both the scale bound (195) and the rotation bound (211). when the true inlier set  $\mathcal{i}$  is known, one can directly compute the bound using eq. (217); when the true inlier set  $\mathcal{i}$  is unknown, one has to enumerate over all possible subsets of 3 inliers in  $\bar{\mathcal{c}}$  to compute the worst-case translation bound.
